For additional verification, set this to data that strongly identifies the value, such as\n41: \t// the key and creation version of the stored data.\n42: \tAuthenticatedData() []byte\n43: }\n44: \n45: type Read interface {\n46: \t// TransformFromStorage may transform the provided data from its underlying storage representation or return an error.\n47: \t// Stale is true if the object on disk is stale and a write to etcd should be issued, even if the contents of the object\n48: \t// have not changed.\n49: \tTransformFromStorage(ctx context.Context, data []byte, dataCtx Context) (out []byte, stale bool, err error)\n50: }\n51: \n52: type Write interface {\n53: \t// TransformToStorage may transform the provided data into the appropriate form in storage or return an error.\n54: \tTransformToStorage(ctx context.Context, data []byte, dataCtx Context) (out []byte, err error)\n55: }\n56: \n57: // Transformer allows a value to be transformed before being read from or written to the underlying store. The methods\n58: // must be able to undo the transformation caused by the other.\n59: type Transformer interface {\n60: \tRead\n61: \tWrite\n62: }\n63: \n64: // ResourceTransformers returns a transformer for the provided resource.\n65: type ResourceTransformers interface {\n66: \tTransformerForResource(resource schema.GroupResource) Transformer\n67: }\n68: \n69: // DefaultContext is a simple implementation of Context for a slice of bytes.\n70: type DefaultContext []byte\n71: \n72: // AuthenticatedData returns itself.\n73: func (c DefaultContext) AuthenticatedData() []byte { return c }\n74: \n75: // PrefixTransformer holds a transformer interface and the prefix that the transformation is located under.\n76: type PrefixTransformer struct {\n77: \tPrefix      []byte\n78: \tTransformer Transformer\n79: }\n80: \n81: type prefixTransformers struct {\n82: \ttransformers []PrefixTransformer\n83: \terr          error\n84: }\n85: \n86: var _ Transformer = &prefixTransformers{}\n87: \n88: // NewPrefixTransformers supports the Transformer interface by checking the incoming data against the provided\n89: // prefixes in order. The first matching prefix will be used to transform the value (the prefix is stripped\n90: // before the Transformer interface is invoked). The first provided transformer will be used when writing to\n91: // the store.\n92: func NewPrefixTransformers(err error, transformers ...PrefixTransformer) Transformer {\n93: \tif err == nil {\n94: \t\terr = fmt.Errorf(\"the provided value does not match any of the supported transformers\")\n95: \t}\n96: \treturn &prefixTransformers{\n97: \t\ttransformers: transformers,\n98: \t\terr:          err,\n99: \t}\n100: }\n101: \n102: // TransformFromStorage finds the first transformer with a prefix matching the provided data and returns\n103: // the result of transforming the value. It will always mark any transformation as stale that is not using\n104: // the first transformer.\n105: func (t *prefixTransformers) TransformFromStorage(ctx context.Context, data []byte, dataCtx Context) ([]byte, bool, error) {\n106: \tstart := time.Now()\n107: \tvar errs []error\n108: \tresource := getResourceFromContext(ctx)\n109: \tfor i, transformer := range t.transformers {\n110: \t\tif bytes.HasPrefix(data, transformer.Prefix) {\n111: \t\t\tresult, stale, err := transformer.Transformer.TransformFromStorage(ctx, data[len(transformer.Prefix):], dataCtx)\n112: \t\t\t// To migrate away from encryption, user can specify an identity transformer higher up\n113: \t\t\t// (in the config file) than the encryption transformer. In that scenario, the identity transformer needs to\n114: \t\t\t// identify (during reads from disk) whether the data being read is encrypted or not. If the data is encrypted,\n115: \t\t\t// it shall throw an error, but that error should not prevent the next subsequent transformer from being tried.\n116: \t\t\tif len(transformer.Prefix) == 0 && err != nil {\n117: \t\t\t\tcontinue\n118: \t\t\t}\n119: \t\t\tif len(transformer.Prefix) == 0 {\n120: \t\t\t\tRecordTransformation(resource, \"from_storage\", \"identity\", time.Since(start), err)\n121: \t\t\t} else {\n122: \t\t\t\tRecordTransformation(resource, \"from_storage\", string(transformer.Prefix), time.Since(start), err)\n123: \t\t\t}\n124: \n125: \t\t\t// It is valid to have overlapping prefixes when the same encryption provider\n126: \t\t\t// is specified multiple times but with different keys (the first provider is\n127: \t\t\t// being rotated to and some later provider is being rotated away from).\n128: \t\t\t//\n129: \t\t\t// Example:\n130: \t\t\t//\n131: \t\t\t//  {\n132: \t\t\t//    \"aescbc\": {\n133: \t\t\t//      \"keys\": [\n134: \t\t\t//        {\n135: \t\t\t//          \"name\": \"2\",\n136: \t\t\t//          \"secret\": \"some key 2\"\n137: \t\t\t//        }\n138: \t\t\t//      ]\n139: \t\t\t//    }\n140: \t\t\t//  },\n141: \t\t\t//  {\n142: \t\t\t//    \"aescbc\": {\n143: \t\t\t//      \"keys\": [\n144: \t\t\t//        {\n145: \t\t\t//          \"name\": \"1\",\n146: \t\t\t//          \"secret\": \"some key 1\"\n147: \t\t\t//        }\n148: \t\t\t//      ]\n149: \t\t\t//    }\n150: \t\t\t//  },\n151: \t\t\t//\n152: \t\t\t// The transformers for both aescbc configs share the prefix k8s:enc:aescbc:v1:\n153: \t\t\t// but a failure in the first one should not prevent a later match from being attempted.\n154: \t\t\t// Thus we never short-circuit on a prefix match that results in an error.\n155: \t\t\tif err != nil {\n156: \t\t\t\terrs = append(errs, err)\n157: \t\t\t\tcontinue\n158: \t\t\t}\n159: \n160: \t\t\treturn result, stale || i != 0, err\n161: \t\t}\n162: \t}\n163: \tif err := errors.Reduce(errors.NewAggregate(errs)); err != nil {\n164: \t\tlogTransformErr(ctx, err, \"failed to decrypt data\")\n165: \t\treturn nil, false, err\n166: \t}\n167: \tRecordTransformation(resource, \"from_storage\", \"unknown\", time.Since(start), t.err)\n168: \treturn nil, false, t.err\n169: }\n170: \n171: // TransformToStorage uses the first transformer and adds its prefix to the data.\n172: func (t *prefixTransformers) TransformToStorage(ctx context.Context, data []byte, dataCtx Context) ([]byte, error) {\n173: \tstart := time.Now()\n174: \ttransformer := t.transformers[0]\n175: \tresource := getResourceFromContext(ctx)\n176: \tresult, err := transformer.Transformer.TransformToStorage(ctx, data, dataCtx)\n177: \tRecordTransformation(resource, \"to_storage\", string(transformer.Prefix), time.Since(start), err)\n178: \tif err != nil {\n179: \t\tlogTransformErr(ctx, err, \"failed to encrypt data\")\n180: \t\treturn nil, err\n181: \t}\n182: \tprefixedData := make([]byte, len(transformer.Prefix), len(result)+len(transformer.Prefix))\n183: \tcopy(prefixedData, transformer.Prefix)\n184: \tprefixedData = append(prefixedData, result...)\n185: \treturn prefixedData, nil\n186: }\n187: \n188: func logTransformErr(ctx context.Context, err error, message string) {\n189: \trequestInfo := getRequestInfoFromContext(ctx)\n190: \tif klogLevel6 := klog.V(6);
